State space parameter configurations.

Multi-scale transformer model for ocean current prediction

The ocean current field exhibits significant spatiotemporal variability, posing challenges for USV path planning. Traditional sensing equipment, such as Acoustic Doppler Current Profilers, provides only single-point instantaneous velocity measurements45, limiting the USV’s ability to perceive the surrounding flow distribution. To address this limitation, a multi-scale Transformer architecture that incorporates physical constraints is proposed for ocean current prediction. This model integrates hierarchical attention mechanisms with fundamental conservation principles, enabling high-resolution local flow field forecasting.

Architecture overview

The prediction model follows an encoder-decoder structure. The global encoder processes the complete ocean current field to extract macroscopic flow patterns, while the local decoder generates high-resolution predictions within a neighborhood of the USV’s current position. The global ocean current field \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{V}_\text {global} \in \mathbb {R}^{2 \times W \times H}$$\end{document}, generated by the potential flow model defined in the Problem Statement section, serves as the primary input.

The global Transformer encoder extracts flow features as:14\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \textbf{F}_\text {global} = \text {Transformer}_\text {global}\left( \textbf{V}_\text {global}, \mathcal {H}_t\right) , \end{aligned}$$\end{document}where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {H}_t$$\end{document} denotes the historical observation buffer and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{F}_\text {global} \in \mathbb {R}^{d}$$\end{document} is the resulting global feature vector of dimension d.

The local decoder predicts the ocean current velocity field \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{V}_f$$\end{document} conditioned on the global features and the USV’s current position:15\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \textbf{V}_f = \text {Transformer}_\text {local}\left( \textbf{F}_\text {global}, \textbf{p}_c, n\right) , \end{aligned}$$\end{document} where n specifies the local prediction window size.

Feature representation

Multi-scale features are constructed through concatenation:16\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} \textbf{S}_t&= \left[ \nu _c(t);\; \textbf{V}_{\text {local},t};\; \textbf{F}_\text {global};\; \sigma (\nu _c(t))\right] , \\ \textbf{X}_t&= \left[ \textbf{p}_t;\; \textbf{a}_t;\; \textbf{S}_t;\; \textbf{PE}_\text {spatial}(\textbf{p}_t);\; \textbf{TE}(t)\right] , \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\nu _c(t)$$\end{document} denotes the ocean current velocity at time t, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{V}_{\text {local},t}$$\end{document} represents historical local observations, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sigma (\cdot )$$\end{document} computes velocity statistics (mean and variance), and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{PE}_\text {spatial}$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{TE}$$\end{document} are spatial and temporal positional encodings, respectively.

Spatiotemporal attention mechanism

A spatiotemporally aware attention mechanism integrates content similarity with spatial-temporal proximity:17\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \alpha _{t,s}^{(i)} = \frac{\exp \left( \dfrac{\textbf{Q}_t^{(i)}(\textbf{K}_s^{(i)})^\top }{\sqrt{d_k}} - \lambda ^{(i)}_1 d_{ts} - \lambda ^{(i)}_2 \Vert \textbf{p}_t - \textbf{p}_s\Vert \right) }{\sum _{s'}\exp \left( \dfrac{\textbf{Q}_t^{(i)}(\textbf{K}_{s'}^{(i)})^\top }{\sqrt{d_k}} - \lambda ^{(i)}_1 d_{ts'} - \lambda ^{(i)}_2 \Vert \textbf{p}_t - \textbf{p}_{s'}\Vert \right) }, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d_{ts} = |t - s|$$\end{document} denotes the temporal distance, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert \textbf{p}_t - \textbf{p}_s\Vert$$\end{document} denotes the spatial distance, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda ^{(i)}_1$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda ^{(i)}_2$$\end{document} are learnable parameters controlling the influence of temporal and spatial proximity in attention head i.

Physics-informed loss function

The loss function incorporates four physically motivated constraints to ensure prediction validity:18\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} \mathcal {L} = \;&\underbrace{\Vert \hat{\textbf{V}}_f - \textbf{V}_\text {local}\Vert _2^2}_{\text {Prediction error}} + \lambda _1\underbrace{\Vert \nabla \cdot \hat{\textbf{V}}_f\Vert _2^2}_{\text {Mass conservation}} \\&+ \lambda _2\underbrace{\Vert \nabla \times \hat{\textbf{V}}_f - \boldsymbol{\omega }\Vert _2^2}_{\text {Vorticity conservation}} + \lambda _3\underbrace{\Vert \hat{\textbf{V}}_f(t) - \hat{\textbf{V}}_f(t-1)\Vert _1}_{\text {Temporal continuity}} \\&+ \lambda _4\underbrace{\mathcal {L}_\text {KL}(\hat{\textbf{V}}_f \Vert \textbf{V}_\text {global})}_{\text {Statistical consistency}}, \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document} where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\hat{\textbf{V}}_f$$\end{document} denotes the predicted velocity field, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{V}_\text {local}$$\end{document} denotes the ground truth, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\nabla \cdot$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\nabla \times$$\end{document} represent the divergence and curl operators, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\boldsymbol{\omega }$$\end{document} is the observed vorticity, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {L}_\text {KL}$$\end{document} is the Kullback-Leibler divergence.

Physical Constraint Implementation. The physical constraints are enforced through soft penalty terms rather than hard architectural constraints, offering three advantages: (1) flexibility to accommodate measurement noise and model uncertainties; (2) end-to-end differentiability for gradient-based optimization; and (3) tunable trade-offs between prediction accuracy and physical consistency.

The hyperparameters are determined through grid search on a validation set:Mass conservation (\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda _1 = 0.1$$\end{document}): Enforces \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\nabla \cdot \textbf{V} \approx 0$$\end{document} for incompressible flow.Vorticity conservation (\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda _2 = 0.1$$\end{document}): Maintains consistency with observed rotational flow patterns.Temporal continuity (\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda _3 = 0.05$$\end{document}): Ensures smooth temporal evolution via L1 regularization.Statistical consistency (\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda _4 = 0.01$$\end{document}): Aligns local predictions with global flow statistics. The effectiveness of these constraints is validated through ablation experiments in Section “Ablation Study on Transformer Physical Constraints”.

Mass conservation (\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda _1 = 0.1$$\end{document}): Enforces \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\nabla \cdot \textbf{V} \approx 0$$\end{document} for incompressible flow.

Mass conservation (\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda _1 = 0.1$$\end{document}): Enforces \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\nabla \cdot \textbf{V} \approx 0$$\end{document} for incompressible flow.

Vorticity conservation (\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda _2 = 0.1$$\end{document}): Maintains consistency with observed rotational flow patterns.

Vorticity conservation (\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda _2 = 0.1$$\end{document}): Maintains consistency with observed rotational flow patterns.

Temporal continuity (\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda _3 = 0.05$$\end{document}): Ensures smooth temporal evolution via L1 regularization.

Temporal continuity (\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda _3 = 0.05$$\end{document}): Ensures smooth temporal evolution via L1 regularization.

Statistical consistency (\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda _4 = 0.01$$\end{document}): Aligns local predictions with global flow statistics.

Statistical consistency (\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda _4 = 0.01$$\end{document}): Aligns local predictions with global flow statistics.

The complete prediction procedure is summarized in Algorithm 1.

Algorithm 1Multi-scale transformer-based ocean current prediction.

Multi-scale transformer-based ocean current prediction.

The predicted ocean current field \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{V}_f$$\end{document} serves as a critical input for both the state representation and the force field computation. The following subsection describes how this information is incorporated into an Enhanced Artificial Potential Field method.

Enhanced artificial potential field

Traditional Artificial Potential Field (APF) methods are susceptible to local minima and exhibit limited adaptability to dynamic environments. To address these limitations, an Enhanced APF (E-APF) is proposed that incorporates three key mechanisms: adaptive force weighting, ocean current integration, and entropy-based escape from local minima.

Composite force field

The total force acting on the USV is computed as a weighted combination of three components:19\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \textbf{F}_{\text {total}} = w_{\text {att}} \textbf{F}_{\text {att}} + w_{\text {rep}} \textbf{F}_{\text {rep}} + w_{\text {flow}} \textbf{F}_{\text {flow}}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{F}_{\text {att}}$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{F}_{\text {rep}}$$\end{document}, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{F}_{\text {flow}}$$\end{document} denote the attractive, repulsive, and current-induced force components, respectively.

The attractive force directs the USV toward the goal:20\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \textbf{F}_{\text {att}} = k_{\text {att}} (\textbf{p}_g - \textbf{p}_c), \end{aligned}$$\end{document} where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$k_{\text {att}}$$\end{document} is the attraction gain coefficient, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{p}_g$$\end{document} is the goal position, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{p}_c$$\end{document} is the current USV position.

The repulsive force prevents collisions with obstacles:21\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \textbf{F}_{\text {rep}} = \sum _{i=1}^{N_{\text {obs}}} k_{\text {rep}}\left( \frac{1}{\Vert \textbf{p}_c-\textbf{p}_{\text {obs}}^i\Vert }-\frac{1}{d_{\text {eff}}}\right) \frac{\hat{\textbf{n}}_i}{\Vert \textbf{p}_c-\textbf{p}_{\text {obs}}^i\Vert ^2}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document} where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{p}_{\text {obs}}^i$$\end{document} denotes the position of the i-th obstacle, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$k_{\text {rep}}$$\end{document} is the repulsion gain, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\hat{\textbf{n}}_i = (\textbf{p}_c - \textbf{p}_{\text {obs}}^i) / \Vert \textbf{p}_c - \textbf{p}_{\text {obs}}^i\Vert$$\end{document} is the unit vector pointing from the obstacle to the USV. The effective repulsion range \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d_{\text {eff}}$$\end{document} is adaptively reduced as the USV approaches the goal:22\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} d_{\text {eff}} = \min \left( d_0,\; \lambda \Vert \textbf{p}_g - \textbf{p}_c\Vert \right) , \end{aligned}$$\end{document} where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d_0$$\end{document} is the maximum repulsion range and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda$$\end{document} is a scaling factor. This adaptation prevents excessive repulsion from interfering with the approach to the goal.

The current-induced force incorporates ocean current effects:23\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \textbf{F}_{\text {flow}} = k_f \nu _c(\textbf{p}_c), \end{aligned}$$\end{document} where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$k_f$$\end{document} is the flow gain coefficient and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\nu _c(\textbf{p}_c)$$\end{document} denotes the ocean current velocity at the USV’s position.

Adaptive weight adjustment

The force weights are dynamically adjusted based on environmental conditions:24\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} w_{\text {att}} = \frac{e^{-\alpha d_{\text {min}}}}{Z}, \quad w_{\text {rep}} = \frac{1-e^{-\beta N_{\text {near}}}}{Z}, \quad w_{\text {flow}} = \frac{\Vert \nu _c\Vert }{Z}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d_{\text {min}}$$\end{document} denotes the distance to the nearest obstacle, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$N_{\text {near}}$$\end{document} denotes the number of obstacles within a sensing radius, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\beta$$\end{document} are sensitivity parameters, and Z is a normalization factor ensuring \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$w_{\text {att}} + w_{\text {rep}} + w_{\text {flow}} = 1$$\end{document}.

Temporal smoothing

To suppress trajectory oscillations caused by rapid force variations, temporal smoothing is applied:25\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \textbf{F}_t = \eta \textbf{F}_{\text {total}} + (1 - \eta ) \textbf{F}_{t-1}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document} where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\eta \in (0,1)$$\end{document} is the smoothing coefficient that blends the current force with the previous timestep’s force.

Probabilistic action selection

Rather than directly following the force vector, E-APF employs probabilistic action selection using a temperature-scaled softmax function. For each candidate action \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{a}_i$$\end{document} in the discrete action space, a score is computed:26\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} S(\textbf{a}_i) = \omega _1 \cos \theta _{Fi} + \omega _2 / d_{\text {proj},i}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\theta _{Fi}$$\end{document} denotes the angle between action \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{a}_i$$\end{document} and the smoothed force \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{F}_t$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d_{\text {proj},i}$$\end{document} denotes the projected distance to the goal along action \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{a}_i$$\end{document}, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega _1$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega _2$$\end{document} are weighting factors.

The action probability is then computed as:27\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} P(\textbf{a}_i) = \frac{\exp (S(\textbf{a}_i)/T)}{\sum _{j=1}^{N_a}\exp (S(\textbf{a}_j)/T)}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document} where the temperature \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$T = T_0 e^{-t/\tau }$$\end{document} decreases exponentially over time, with initial temperature \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$T_0$$\end{document} and decay constant \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tau$$\end{document}. This annealing schedule transitions from exploration (high T) to exploitation (low T).

Entropy-based escape mechanism

Local minima occur when attractive and repulsive forces approximately cancel, leaving no clear preferred direction. This situation manifests as high entropy in the action probability distribution. The entropy is computed as:28\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} H(\mathcal {A}) = -\sum _{i=1}^{N_a} P(\textbf{a}_i) \log P(\textbf{a}_i). \end{aligned}$$\end{document} When \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$H(\mathcal {A}) > H_{\text {threshold}}$$\end{document}, indicating potential entrapment, the algorithm temporarily selects random actions from a safe action set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {A}_{\text {safe}}$$\end{document}(actions that do not lead to immediate collision) to escape the local minimum.

The complete E-APF procedure is summarized in Algorithm 2.

Algorithm 2Enhanced artificial potential field path planning.

Enhanced artificial potential field path planning.

While E-APF provides physics-based navigation guidance, the DQN component requires a carefully designed reward function to learn effective policies. The following subsection presents the hierarchical reward framework.

Reward function

The reward function is a critical component that directly shapes the learned navigation policy. A hierarchical reward framework is proposed, comprising four modules: task completion, path efficiency, environmental adaptation, and exploration. The total reward at each time step is:29\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} R_{\text {total}} = \sum _{i=1}^{4} w_i \cdot r_i(s_t, a_t, s_{t+1}), \end{aligned}$$\end{document} where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$w_i$$\end{document} denotes the weight for the i-th reward component.

Task completion reward

The task-oriented reward provides sparse signals for goal achievement and collision avoidance:30\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} r_{\text {task}} = {\left\{ \begin{array}{ll} R_{\text {goal}}, & \text {if } \Vert \textbf{p}_c - \textbf{p}_g\Vert \le \epsilon \\ -R_{\text {collision}}, & \text {if collision detected} \\ 0, & \text {otherwise} \end{array}\right. } \end{aligned}$$\end{document} where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$R_{\text {goal}}$$\end{document} denotes the goal achievement reward, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$R_{\text {collision}}$$\end{document} denotes the collision penalty, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\epsilon$$\end{document} is the goal proximity threshold.

Path efficiency reward

Dense rewards encourage progress toward the goal and trajectory smoothness:31\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} r_{\text {dist}}&= \alpha (d_t - d_{t+1}), \\ r_{\text {smooth}}&= -\beta _1 |\theta _t - \theta _{t-1}| - \beta _2 \Vert \dot{\textbf{p}}_t - \dot{\textbf{p}}_{t-1}\Vert , \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d_t = \Vert \textbf{p}_c - \textbf{p}_g\Vert$$\end{document} denotes the distance to the goal at time t, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\theta _t$$\end{document} denotes the heading angle, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\dot{\textbf{p}}_t$$\end{document} denotes the USV velocity. The distance reduction reward \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r_{\text {dist}}$$\end{document} encourages goal approach, while \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r_{\text {smooth}}$$\end{document} penalizes abrupt heading and velocity changes.

Environmental adaptation reward

This module encourages current utilization and obstacle avoidance:32\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} r_{\text {curr}}&= \gamma \max (0, \cos \theta _{\text {ac}}) \cdot (1 + \tanh \Vert \nu _c\Vert ), \\ r_{\text {safe}}&= \delta _1 \frac{DT(\textbf{p}_c)}{\max (DT)} - \delta _2 \exp \left( -\frac{\min _{i}\Vert \textbf{p}_c - \textbf{p}_{\text {obs}}^i\Vert }{d_{\text {safe}}}\right) , \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document} where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\theta _{\text {ac}}$$\end{document} denotes the angle between the USV heading and the current direction, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\nu _c$$\end{document} denotes the ocean current velocity, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$DT(\textbf{p}_c)$$\end{document} denotes the distance transform value at the current position (larger values indicate greater distance from obstacles), and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d_{\text {safe}}$$\end{document} is a safety distance parameter. The current alignment reward \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r_{\text {curr}}$$\end{document} encourages energy-efficient navigation by utilizing favorable currents, while \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r_{\text {safe}}$$\end{document} rewards maintaining safe distances from obstacles.

Exploration and energy reward

This module balances exploration incentives with energy consumption penalties:33\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} r_{\text {exp}}&= \eta _1 \mathbb {I}(\textbf{p}_c \notin \mathcal {T}) + \eta _2 \exp (-\Vert \nabla \Phi _{\text {exp}}\Vert ), \\ r_{\text {energy}}&= -\lambda \Vert \dot{\textbf{p}}_t - \nu _c(\textbf{p}_c)\Vert , \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document} where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {T}$$\end{document} denotes the set of previously visited positions, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {I}(\cdot )$$\end{document} denotes the indicator function, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Phi _{\text {exp}}$$\end{document} denotes the exploration potential field encoding spatial coverage information, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert \dot{\textbf{p}}_t - \nu _c\Vert$$\end{document} represents the relative velocity magnitude (proportional to propulsion effort). The first term of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r_{\text {exp}}$$\end{document} rewards visiting new areas, while the second term encourages movement toward regions with lower exploration potential gradients. The energy penalty \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r_{\text {energy}}$$\end{document} discourages excessive propulsion effort.

Reward function weights and parameters.

With the reward function defined, the next subsection presents the enhanced DQN architecture that learns navigation policies through interaction with the environment.

Deep Q-network with median-based exploration

This study adopts an enhanced Deep Q-Network (DQN) architecture to improve decision-making capabilities in complex marine environments. The proposed algorithm incorporates four key mechanisms: Dueling network structure, prioritized experience replay, adaptive soft update, and a novel median Q-value based exploration strategy.

Dueling network architecture

The Dueling DQN decomposes the state-action value function Q(s, a) into state value and action advantage components46:34\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} Q(s,a) = V(s) + \left( A(s,a) - \frac{1}{|\mathcal {A}|}\sum _{a'}A(s,a')\right) , \end{aligned}$$\end{document}where V(s) represents the state value function and A(s, a) denotes the action advantage function. This decomposition enables independent learning of state values and action advantages, resulting in more accurate and robust value estimation.

Prioritized experience replay

To enhance sample efficiency, prioritized experience replay47 is employed. For each transition \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(s_t, a_t, r_t, s_{t+1})$$\end{document}, the sampling probability is determined by the TD error:35\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} P(i) = \frac{p_i^\alpha }{\sum _k p_k^\alpha }, \quad p_i = |\delta _i| + \varepsilon , \end{aligned}$$\end{document} where the TD error is computed as:36\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \delta _i = r_i + \gamma \max _{a'}Q(s_{i+1},a';\theta ') - Q(s_i,a_i;\theta ), \end{aligned}$$\end{document}\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha$$\end{document} controls the degree of prioritization, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varepsilon$$\end{document} is a small constant ensuring non-zero sampling probabilities.

Importance sampling weights correct for the bias introduced by non-uniform sampling:37\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} w_i = \frac{(N \cdot P(i))^{-\beta }}{\max _j w_j}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document} where N denotes the replay buffer size and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\beta$$\end{document} gradually increases from an initial value to 1 during training, progressively correcting the sampling bias.

The network is optimized using a weighted TD loss:38\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathcal {L}(\theta ) = \mathbb {E}_{(s,a,r,s') \sim \mathcal {D}}\left[ w_i\left( r + \gamma \max _{a'}Q(s',a';\theta ') - Q(s,a;\theta )\right) ^2\right] . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}

Adaptive soft update

The target network update rate is dynamically adjusted based on learning progress48:39\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \tau = \tau _{\min } + (\tau _{\max } - \tau _{\min }) \cdot \text {sigmoid}(\overline{|\delta |}), \end{aligned}$$\end{document}where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\overline{|\delta |}$$\end{document} denotes the mean absolute TD error of the current batch. The target network parameters are updated as:40\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \theta ' \leftarrow \tau \theta + (1 - \tau )\theta '. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}This adaptive mechanism increases the update rate when TD errors are large (indicating significant learning potential) and decreases it when errors are small (prioritizing stability).

Median Q-value based exploration

The standard \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\epsilon$$\end{document}-greedy strategy selects random actions uniformly from the entire action space:41\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} a_t = {\left\{ \begin{array}{ll} \text {random}(\mathcal {A}), & \text {if } \xi < \epsilon \\ \mathop {\mathrm {arg\,max}}\limits _{a}Q(s_t,a), & \text {otherwise} \end{array}\right. } \end{aligned}$$\end{document} Although simple, this approach wastes exploration resources on clearly suboptimal actions.

To address this limitation, a median Q-value based exploration mechanism is proposed:42\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} a_t = {\left\{ \begin{array}{ll} \text {random}(\mathcal {A}_{\text {mid}}), & \text {if } \xi < \epsilon \\ \mathop {\mathrm {arg\,max}}\limits _{a}Q(s_t,a), & \text {otherwise} \end{array}\right. } \end{aligned}$$\end{document}where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {A}_{\text {mid}}$$\end{document} contains actions with Q-values in the interquartile range:43\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathcal {A}_{\text {mid}} = \{a_i \mid Q_{25\%}(s_t) \le Q(s_t,a_i) \le Q_{75\%}(s_t)\}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Here, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Q_{25\%}(s_t)$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Q_{75\%}(s_t)$$\end{document} denote the 25th and 75th percentiles of Q-values across all actions in state \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$s_t$$\end{document}.

Theoretical Motivation. The median-Q exploration strategy is motivated by three observations specific to USV path planning: Structured Q-value Distribution: In the 8-direction discrete action space, Q-values exhibit characteristic patterns. High-Q actions (top 25%) correspond to goal-directed movements aligned with favorable currents, which are already exploited during greedy selection. Low-Q actions (bottom 25%) represent clearly suboptimal choices where exploration provides minimal learning value.Information Gain Efficiency: Medium-Q actions (25%–75%) represent the region of highest uncertainty where exploration yields maximum information gain. Standard \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\epsilon$$\end{document}-greedy allocates exploration uniformly, including low-Q actions unlikely to improve policy quality. Median-Q exploration redirects samples to actions with higher potential for value refinement.Accelerated Convergence: By excluding extreme Q-values from exploration, the strategy maintains meaningful exploration of promising alternatives while avoiding detrimental actions, as demonstrated in Section “Analysis of the Effectiveness of the Median-based Exploration Strategy”.Potential Bias Mitigation. Three mechanisms address the theoretical concern that median-Q exploration may bias against initially underestimated optimal actions:Q-value Dynamics: Underestimated optimal actions receive positive TD updates when selected via the greedy component (\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$1-\epsilon$$\end{document} probability), gradually moving into the explorable range.APF Guidance: The E-APF component provides physics-based action preferences independent of Q-values, offering an alternative pathway for discovering optimal actions through the policy fusion mechanism.Reward Structure: The hierarchical reward function ensures that beneficial actions (goal-directed, current-aligned, obstacle-avoiding) quickly accumulate positive returns, preventing them from remaining in the bottom 25% for extended periods.

Structured Q-value Distribution: In the 8-direction discrete action space, Q-values exhibit characteristic patterns. High-Q actions (top 25%) correspond to goal-directed movements aligned with favorable currents, which are already exploited during greedy selection. Low-Q actions (bottom 25%) represent clearly suboptimal choices where exploration provides minimal learning value.

Low-Q actions (bottom 25%) represent clearly suboptimal choices where exploration provides minimal learning value.

Information Gain Efficiency: Medium-Q actions (25%–75%) represent the region of highest uncertainty where exploration yields maximum information gain. Median-Q exploration redirects samples to actions with higher potential for value refinement.

Median-Q exploration redirects samples to actions with higher potential for value refinement.

Accelerated Convergence: By excluding extreme Q-values from exploration, the strategy maintains meaningful exploration of promising alternatives while avoiding detrimental actions, as demonstrated in Section “Analysis of the Effectiveness of the Median-based Exploration Strategy”.

Reward Structure: The hierarchical reward function ensures that beneficial actions (goal-directed, current-aligned, obstacle-avoiding) quickly accumulate positive returns, preventing them from remaining in the bottom 25% for extended periods.

Reward Structure: The hierarchical reward function ensures that beneficial actions (goal-directed, current-aligned, obstacle-avoiding) quickly accumulate positive returns, preventing them from remaining in the bottom 25% for extended periods.

APF-guided deep Q-network integration

Having introduced the E-APF method and the enhanced DQN algorithm separately, this subsection describes how these components are integrated into a unified framework. A multi-level integration approach is established through three complementary mechanisms: state space augmentation, APF-guided loss function, and policy fusion.

State space augmentation

The state representation is augmented with potential field information to enhance environmental awareness:44\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} s_t^{\text {aug}} = [s_t;\; \textbf{F}_{\text {total}};\; \nabla \textbf{F}_{\text {total}}], \end{aligned}$$\end{document} where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{F}_{\text {total}}$$\end{document} denotes the total force vector computed by E-APF at the current position, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\nabla \textbf{F}_{\text {total}}$$\end{document} denotes its spatial gradient. This augmentation provides the DQN with explicit information about the force field topology, enabling physics-informed policy learning.

APF-guided loss function

The Q-network training is regularized to align with E-APF guidance through a composite loss function:45\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathcal {L}_{\text {total}} = \mathcal {L}_{\text {DQN}} + \lambda _{\text {APF}} \left\| \textbf{q}(s) - \alpha \textbf{F}_{\text {total}} \right\| _2^2, \end{aligned}$$\end{document} where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {L}_{\text {DQN}}$$\end{document} denotes the standard TD loss, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{q}(s) = [Q(s,a_1), \ldots , Q(s,a_{|\mathcal {A}|})]^\top$$\end{document} denotes the Q-value vector across all actions. This term encourages the Q-value landscape to reflect the structure of the potential field, with higher Q-values for actions aligned with the E-APF force direction.

Design Rationale. The \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L_2$$\end{document} norm is chosen for its convexity and differentiability, which provide stable gradient signals that encourage the learned Q-value distribution to align with the physics-based potential field direction. This formulation effectively embeds physical prior knowledge into the reinforcement learning process, accelerating early convergence.

Hyperparameter Selection. The scaling factor \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha = 0.1$$\end{document} was determined empirically to align the magnitude of force vectors (\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|\textbf{F}_{\text {total}}| \approx 1$$\end{document}–10) with Q-value estimates (\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|Q| \approx 100$$\end{document}–400). The regularization weight \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda _{\text {APF}} = 0.01$$\end{document} was selected to balance early-stage physics guidance with later-stage reward-driven learning, ensuring that the APF term provides meaningful guidance without dominating the TD loss.

Policy fusion

The final action distribution is obtained by blending DQN and E-APF policies:46\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} P(a_i) = (1 - w_{\text {APF}}) P_{\text {DQN}}(a_i) + w_{\text {APF}} P_{\text {E-APF}}(a_i), \end{aligned}$$\end{document} where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$P_{\text {DQN}}(a_i) = \text {softmax}(Q(s,a_i)/T_Q)$$\end{document} is derived from Q-values, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$P_{\text {E-APF}}(a_i) = \text {softmax}(S(a_i)/T_F)$$\end{document} is derived from E-APF action scores defined in the E-APF subsection. The temperatures \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$T_Q$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$T_F$$\end{document} control the sharpness of each distribution.

The fusion weight \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$w_{\text {APF}}$$\end{document} adapts based on training progress and policy confidence:47\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} w_{\text {APF}} = \max \left( 0, 1 - \frac{t}{T_{\text {decay}}}\right) \cdot \exp \left( -\frac{\text {Var}(\textbf{q}(s))}{\sigma _w}\right) , \end{aligned}$$\end{document} where t denotes the current training step, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$T_{\text {decay}}$$\end{document} denotes the decay horizon, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\text {Var}(\textbf{q}(s))$$\end{document} denotes the variance of Q-values across actions (indicating confidence), and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sigma _w$$\end{document} is a sensitivity parameter.

Design Rationale. The adaptive weight schedule incorporates two complementary principles: Temporal Decay: The term \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\max (0, 1-t/T_{\text {decay}})$$\end{document} ensures that E-APF guidance dominates during early training when Q-estimates are unreliable, then gradually transfers control to the learned DQN policy as training progresses.Confidence-Based Adjustment: The term \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\exp (-\text {Var}(\textbf{q})/\sigma _w)$$\end{document} reduces APF influence when Q-value variance is high (indicating confident action preferences) and increases it when variance is low (indicating uncertainty). This allows the DQN to override APF guidance in states where it has learned reliable value estimates. The effectiveness of this integration is validated in Section “APF-DQN Integration Framework”, where APF-DQN achieves 31.4% faster convergence compared to standalone DQN (\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$42.67 \pm 1.37$$\end{document} vs. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$62.25 \pm 1.14$$\end{document} episodes, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$p < 0.001$$\end{document}).

Temporal Decay: The term \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\max (0, 1-t/T_{\text {decay}})$$\end{document} ensures that E-APF guidance dominates during early training when Q-estimates are unreliable, then gradually transfers control to the learned DQN policy as training progresses.

Temporal Decay: The term \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\max (0, 1-t/T_{\text {decay}})$$\end{document} ensures that E-APF guidance dominates during early training when Q-estimates are unreliable, then gradually transfers control to the learned DQN policy as training progresses.

Confidence-Based Adjustment: The term \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\exp (-\text {Var}(\textbf{q})/\sigma _w)$$\end{document} reduces APF influence when Q-value variance is high (indicating confident action preferences) and increases it when variance is low (indicating uncertainty). This allows the DQN to override APF guidance in states where it has learned reliable value estimates.

Confidence-Based Adjustment: The term \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\exp (-\text {Var}(\textbf{q})/\sigma _w)$$\end{document} reduces APF influence when Q-value variance is high (indicating confident action preferences) and increases it when variance is low (indicating uncertainty). This allows the DQN to override APF guidance in states where it has learned reliable value estimates.

The complete APF-guided DQN algorithm is summarized in Algorithm 3.

Experiments

This section presents a comprehensive experimental evaluation of the proposed APF-DQN framework. The experimental setup is first described, including the simulation environment, comparative algorithms, evaluation metrics, and reproducibility measures. The main experimental results are then presented, followed by ablation studies that analyze the contribution of individual components. Finally, the robustness and generalization capability of APF-DQN are evaluated, and its limitations are discussed.

APF-guided deep Q-network algorithm.

Experimental setup

Simulation environment

A \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$40 \times 40$$\end{document} two-dimensional grid environment is constructed to simulate realistic marine navigation conditions for USV path planning. The simulation framework comprises three core components:Obstacle Configuration: The environment contains multiple rectangular obstacles and a central circular obstacle, collectively occupying 25% of the total area. This configuration mimics common navigational hazards encountered in maritime operations.Ocean Current Field: A dual-vortex current system is generated through the superposition of two counter-rotating vortex fields, producing a dynamic and irregular flow regime with peak velocities reaching 5.0 m/s.Navigation Task: The USV is required to navigate from the starting position at coordinates (35, 2) to the goal position at (5, 35) within a maximum of 200 decision steps.Figure 5 illustrates the simulation environment, highlighting the obstacle layout and ocean current patterns. All comparative experiments are conducted under identical environmental configurations and initial conditions to ensure fair evaluation.Fig. 5Simulation environment for USV path planning. Black regions represent fixed obstacles, colored arrows indicate ocean current intensity and direction, the blue dot marks the starting position, and the green star denotes the goal position. The red dashed line shows a typical path generated by the traditional APF method, while the cyan solid line represents the path produced by the proposed APF-DQN method.

Comparative algorithms

To systematically assess the contributions of different components in APF-DQN, the proposed method is compared with the following path planning algorithms:T-APF: The traditional Artificial Potential Field method serves as the baseline approach. This method operates without considering ocean current effects.E-APF: This enhanced APF method incorporates ocean current influences through dynamically weighted potential fields, as described in the Method section.DQN: This standard DQN implementation shares identical network architecture and reward mechanisms with APF-DQN, enabling direct comparison of the integration framework’s contribution.DQN-EG: This DQN variant employs the conventional \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\epsilon$$\end{document}-greedy exploration strategy instead of the proposed median-based exploration, isolating the effect of the exploration mechanism.APF-DQN-NOC: This variant of APF-DQN excludes ocean current consideration, isolating the impact of environmental dynamics on path planning performance. Evaluation metrics

A comprehensive set of metrics is designed to evaluate algorithm performance across three aspects: safety and efficiency, energy consumption, and learning performance.

Safety and efficiency metrics

The following metrics assess the safety and efficiency of path planning:Success Rate: The proportion of episodes in which the USV successfully reaches the goal, reflecting overall task completion capability.Path Length: The total distance traveled by the USV, measuring path optimization performance.Decision Steps: The number of actions required to complete the navigation task, indicating decision-making efficiency.Convergence Speed: The number of training episodes required for policy stabilization. Convergence is defined as achieving stable rewards above a predefined threshold for 10 consecutive episodes.Energy consumption metrics

Success Rate: The proportion of episodes in which the USV successfully reaches the goal, reflecting overall task completion capability.

Success Rate: The proportion of episodes in which the USV successfully reaches the goal, reflecting overall task completion capability.

Path Length: The total distance traveled by the USV, measuring path optimization performance.

Path Length: The total distance traveled by the USV, measuring path optimization performance.

Decision Steps: The number of actions required to complete the navigation task, indicating decision-making efficiency.

Decision Steps: The number of actions required to complete the navigation task, indicating decision-making efficiency.

Convergence Speed: The number of training episodes required for policy stabilization. Convergence is defined as achieving stable rewards above a predefined threshold for 10 consecutive episodes.

Convergence Speed: The number of training episodes required for policy stabilization. Convergence is defined as achieving stable rewards above a predefined threshold for 10 consecutive episodes.

The USV’s energy consumption is modeled as the sum of three components: Basic Movement Energy: \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$E_{\text {base}} = k_{\text {base}} \cdot d$$\end{document}, where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$k_{\text {base}}$$\end{document} is the unit energy cost per distance and d is the traveled distance.Turning Energy: \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$E_{\text {turn}} = k_{\text {turn}} \cdot \min (\Delta \theta , 2\pi - \Delta \theta )$$\end{document}, where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$k_{\text {turn}}$$\end{document} is the turning energy coefficient and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Delta \theta$$\end{document} is the heading change.Ocean Current Effect: \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$E_{\text {current}} = -k_{\text {current}} \cdot \cos \alpha \cdot \Vert \textbf{v}_c\Vert \cdot d$$\end{document}, where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$k_{\text {current}}$$\end{document} is the current interaction coefficient, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha$$\end{document} is the angle between the USV heading and the current direction, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{v}_c$$\end{document} is the current velocity. This term is negative when the USV moves with the current (energy saving) and positive when moving against it (additional consumption).Learning performance metrics

Basic Movement Energy: \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$E_{\text {base}} = k_{\text {base}} \cdot d$$\end{document}, where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$k_{\text {base}}$$\end{document} is the unit energy cost per distance and d is the traveled distance.

This term is negative when the USV moves with the current (energy saving) and positive when moving against it (additional consumption).
